The author thanks the members of the CERMICS-LCT working group on accelerated MD methods, whose discussions motivated this work;
in particular, Gabriel Stoltz and Tony Lelièvre for feedback on early versions of the method, and Pierre Monmarché for remarks which inspired Definition 3.
Appendix A Proof of Theorem 7
In the following, the constants ε0,C>0\varepsilon_{0},C>0 denote ε\varepsilon-independent quantities, whose values may be respectively reduced or increased from line to line. To study ℒε\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon} perturbatively, we consider the rescaled nonnegative operator
T⁡(ε)=−ε​ℒεX=−ℒmic−ε⁡(ℒmac−K)=−(R+ε​S)T(\varepsilon)=-\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{X}=-\mathcal{L}_{\rm mic}-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}-K\right)=-(R+\varepsilon S)
(202)
as a perturbation of the reducible operator −ℒmic=−R-\mathcal{L}_{\rm mic}=-R. We denote by (ηj​(ε))1≤j≤|𝒳|(\eta_{j}(\varepsilon))_{1\leq j\leq|\mathcal{X}|} the eigenvalues of T⁡(ε)T(\varepsilon), enumerated with algebraic multiplicity in order of non-decreasing real part.
Projector and semigroup perturbations.
We first recall some basic tools from perturbation theory (see [34] for a comprehensive discussion). We consider a continuous family of operators (Hε)0≤ε<ε0(H_{\varepsilon})_{0\leq\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}} on ℓ∞​(𝒳)\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}) satisfying ‖Hε−H0‖≤C​ε\|H_{\varepsilon}-H_{0}\|\leq C\varepsilon for some constants C,ε0C,\varepsilon_{0} and all 0≤ε<ε00\leq\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}.
All the results we use can be derived from the Dunford formula [34, Section I.5.6], which states that for any linear operator HH and a function ff holomorphic on a neighborhood of Γ\Gamma and its interior,
f⁡(H)​PH​(Γ)=12​i​π​∮Γf⁡(z)​(z−H)−1​𝑑z,f(H)P_{H}(\Gamma)=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\Gamma}f(z)(z-H)^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}z,
(203)
where Γ\Gamma is a closed, positively oriented contour in ℂ\mathbb{C} not intersecting σ⁡(H)\sigma(H), and PH​(Γ)P_{H}(\Gamma) is the spectral projector associated to the part of σ⁡(H)\sigma(H) enclosed by Γ\Gamma.
Since σ⁡(Hε)\sigma(H_{\varepsilon}) depends continuously on ε\varepsilon, we may assume (upon reducing ε0\varepsilon_{0}) that Γ\Gamma does not intersect any of the σ⁡(Hε)\sigma(H_{\varepsilon}) for ε<ε0\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}, so that
supz∈Γ,0≤ε<ε0​‖(z−Hε)−1‖=M<∞.{\underset{z\in\Gamma,0\leq\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}}{\sup}\,\|(z-H_{\varepsilon})^{-1}\|=M<\infty}.
(204)
Writing Pε​(Γ)P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma) for the spectral projector of HεH_{\varepsilon}, we have
Pε​(Γ)−P0​(Γ)\displaystyle P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)-P_{0}(\Gamma)
=12​i​π​∮Γ[(z−Hε)−1−(z−H0)−1]​𝑑z\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\Gamma}\left[(z-H_{\varepsilon})^{-1}-(z-H_{0})^{-1}\right]\,\mathrm{d}z
(205)
=12​i​π​∮Γ(z−Hε)−1​(Hε−H0)​(z−H0)−1​𝑑z\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\Gamma}(z-H_{\varepsilon})^{-1}(H_{\varepsilon}-H_{0})(z-H_{0})^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}z
so that estimating, we obtain
‖Pε​(Γ)−P0​(Γ)‖≤C​M2​ℓ​(Γ)2​π​ε\|P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)-P_{0}(\Gamma)\|\leq\frac{CM^{2}\ell(\Gamma)}{2\pi}\varepsilon
(206)
with ℓ⁡(Γ)\ell(\Gamma) being the contour length.
In particular, the projector-valued map ε↦Pε​(Γ)\varepsilon\mapsto P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma) is continuous (even locally Lipschitz) at ε=0\varepsilon=0.
Moreover, the projectors Pε​(Γ)P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma) and P0​(Γ)P_{0}(\Gamma) are conjugated for ε<ε0\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0} via
Pε​(Γ)=Uε−1​P0​(Γ)​Uε,‖Uε−Id‖≤C​‖Pε−P0‖≤C​ε,P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)=U_{\varepsilon}^{-1}P_{0}(\Gamma)U_{\varepsilon},\qquad\|U_{\varepsilon}-\mathrm{Id}\|\leq C\|P_{\varepsilon}-P_{0}\|\leq C\varepsilon,
(207)
where UεU_{\varepsilon} is an isomorphism mapping Pε​(Γ)​ℓ∞​(𝒳)→P0​(Γ)​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})\to P_{0}(\Gamma)\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}). In particular, rank​Pε​(Γ)=rank​P0​(Γ){\rm rank}\,P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)={\rm rank}\,P_{0}(\Gamma).
The operator UεU_{\varepsilon} is a construction of Kato, see [34, Section I.4.6], which we will call Kato’s connection.
If Γ1\Gamma_{1} and Γ2\Gamma_{2} are disjoint closed contours enclosed in Γ\Gamma, whose union encloses the Γ\Gamma-part of σ⁡(Hε)\sigma(H_{\varepsilon}), then for ε<ε0\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0},
Pε​(Γ)=Pε​(Γ1)+Pε​(Γ2),Pε​(Γ1)​Pε​(Γ2)=Pε​(Γ2)​Pε​(Γ1)=0.P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)=P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{1})+P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{2}),\qquad P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{1})P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{2})=P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{2})P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{1})=0.
(208)
Taking Γ\Gamma large enough for HεH_{\varepsilon} bounded gives the case Pε​(Γ)=IdP_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)=\mathrm{Id}.
If Γ⊂{z∈ℂ:ℜ​𝔢​z<−h<0}{\Gamma\subset\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\,\mathfrak{Re}\,z<-h<0\}}, we have the semigroup estimate
∀t≥0,‖eHε​t​Pε​(Γ)‖=‖12​i​π​∮Γez​t​(z−Hε)−1​𝑑z‖≤M​ℓ​(Γ)2​π​e−h​t.\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\left\|\mathrm{e}^{H_{\varepsilon}t}P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\Gamma}e^{zt}(z-H_{\varepsilon})^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}z\right\|\leq\frac{M\ell(\Gamma)}{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{-ht}.
(209)
Clustering of σ⁡(T⁡(ε))\sigma(T(\varepsilon)).
We use Kato’s reduction process to study perturbations of small eigenvalues of T⁡(ε)T(\varepsilon).
The operator T⁡(0)=−RT(0)=-R is a direct sum (see Equation (121)), and each of its blocks AmA_{m} is the generator of an irreducible Markov jump process. In particular σ⁡(−T⁡(0))=⋃mσ⁡(Am)\sigma(-T(0))=\bigcup_{m}\sigma(A_{m}). The Perron–Frobenius theorem implies that 00 is a simple eigenvalue of AmA_{m}, hence a semisimple eigenvalue of T⁡(0)T(0) with multiplicity MM.
We introduce the following quantity
γf=min1≤m≤M​min⁡ℜ​𝔢​(σ⁡(−Am)∖{0}).\gamma^{\rm f}=\underset{1\leq m\leq M}{\min}\min\,{\mathfrak{Re}}\left(\sigma(-A_{m})\setminus\{0\}\right).
(210)
We introduce two disjoint closed contours Γs\Gamma^{\rm s} and Γf\Gamma^{\rm f}, enclosing 00 and σ⁡(−T⁡(0))∖{0}{\sigma(-T(0))\setminus\{0\}} respectively, such that
Γf⊂{z∈ℂ:ℜez<−γf/2}.\Gamma^{\rm f}\subset\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\,{\mathfrak{R}e}\,z<-\gamma^{\rm f}/2\}.
(211)
We use the shorthand Pεs=Pε​(Γs)P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}=P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma^{\rm s}) and Pεf=Pε​(Γf)P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm f}=P_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma^{\rm f}).
A schematic representation of σ⁡(−T⁡(ε))\sigma(-T(\varepsilon)), Γs\Gamma^{\rm s} and Γf\Gamma^{\rm f} is given in the left panel of Figure 18.
We first study the Γs\Gamma^{\rm s}-part of σ⁡(−T⁡(ε))\sigma(-T(\varepsilon)). Since 00 is a semisimple eigenvalue of −R-R, [34, Section II.5.4, Theorem 5.4] shows that ηj​(ε)\eta_{j}(\varepsilon) is positively semi-differentiable at ε=0\varepsilon=0 for each 1≤j≤M1\leq j\leq M, with an expansion
∀ 1≤j≤M,ηj​(ε)=ε​ηj(1)+𝒪⁡(ε),\forall\,1\leq j\leq M,\qquad\eta_{j}(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon\eta_{j}^{(1)}+\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathcal{O}$}(\varepsilon),
(212)
where the ηj(1)\eta_{j}^{(1)} are the eigenvalues of the reduced perturbation
T(1)=−P0s​S​P0s.T^{(1)}=-P_{0}^{\rm s}SP_{0}^{\rm s}.
(213)
Note that the fiber-decomposition (121) gives in this case an explicit formula
P0s=∑m=1M𝟙𝒳m​μm.P_{0}^{\rm s}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m}}\mu_{m}.
(214)
We order the (ηj(1))1≤j≤M(\eta_{j}^{(1)})_{1\leq j\leq M} by non-decreasing real part, and characterize them more precisely by noticing that −T(1)-T^{(1)} generates a conditionally irreducible sub-Markovian semigroup on P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}).
We work in the basis {𝟙𝒳m, 1≤m≤M}\{\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m}},\,1\leq m\leq M\} of P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}), with respect to which −T(1)-T^{(1)} has the matrix representation
−Tm,m′(1)=μm​(ℒmac−K)​𝟙𝒳m′,∀ 1≤m,m′≤M.-T^{(1)}_{m,m^{\prime}}=\mu_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}-K)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}},\qquad\forall\,1\leq m,m^{\prime}\leq M.
(215)
Since K​𝟙𝒳m′K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}} is supported on 𝒳m′\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}} and μm\mu_{m} is supported on 𝒳m\mathcal{X}_{m}, for m≠m′m\neq m^{\prime}, we have
−Tm,m′(1)=μm​ℒmac​𝟙𝒳m′=∑x∈𝒳m∑x′∈𝒳m′μm​(x)​ℒmac​(x,x′)​𝟙𝒳m′​(x′)≥0,-T^{(1)}_{m,m^{\prime}}=\mu_{m}\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{m}}\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}}\mu_{m}(x)\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})\geq 0,
(216)
since x≠x′x\neq x^{\prime} in the summand and ℒmac\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac} generates a jump process on 𝒳\mathcal{X}. In (216), ℒmac​(x,x′)\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}(x,x^{\prime}) denotes the (x,x′)(x,x^{\prime})-th entry in the canonical matrix representation of ℒmac\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}.
Since ℒmac​𝟙𝒳=0\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=0, we have for any 1≤m≤M1\leq m\leq M,
−∑m′=1MTm,m′(1)=μm((ℒmac−K)𝟙𝒳)=−μm(K𝟙𝒳)≤0,-\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{M}T^{(1)}_{m,m^{\prime}}=\mu_{m}((\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}-K)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}})=-\mu_{m}(K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}})\leq 0,
(217)
since KK is nonnegative. Therefore, −T(1)-T^{(1)} generates a sub-Markovian semigroup on P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}).
It remains to check the conditional irreducibility of −T(1)-T^{(1)}. We say x≠x′∈𝒳x\neq x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X} are neighbors if ℒmac​(x,x′)>0\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}(x,x^{\prime})>0.
First, notice that if x∈𝒳mx\in\mathcal{X}_{m} and x′∈𝒳m′x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}} are neighbors for m≠m′m\neq m^{\prime}, then the formula (216) implies that −Tm,m′(1)>0-T^{(1)}_{m,m^{\prime}}>0. Indeed, μm​(x)>0\mu_{m}(x)>0 for every x∈𝒳mx\in\mathcal{X}_{m} (by the Perron–Frobenius theorem applied to AmA_{m}), and each summand in (216) is nonnegative.
Let 1≤m≠m′≤M1\leq m\neq m^{\prime}\leq M and x∈𝒳mx\in\mathcal{X}_{m}, x′∈𝒳m′x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}. Since ℒmac\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac} is irreducible, there exists a chain of neighbors x′=x1≠⋯≠xp=xx^{\prime}=x_{1}\neq\dots\neq x_{p}=x in 𝒳\mathcal{X}.
From this chain, we extract the sequence of jumps between fibers, ((xik,xik+1))1≤k≤q\left((x_{i_{k}},x_{i_{k}+1})\right)_{1\leq k\leq q}, where 1≤ik<p1\leq i_{k}<p, ξ⁡(xik)≠ξ⁡(xik+1)\xi(x_{i_{k}})\neq\xi(x_{i_{k}+1}), ξ⁡(xik+1)=ξ⁡(xik+1)\xi(x_{i_{k}+1})=\xi(x_{i_{k+1}}) and ξ⁡(xi1)=m′\xi(x_{i_{1}})=m^{\prime}, ξ⁡(xiq+1)=m\xi(x_{i_{q}+1})=m.
Since xikx_{i_{k}} and xik+1x_{i_{k}+1} are neighbors in distinct fibers, by the previous remark, we have identified a chain (−Tξ⁡(xik),ξ⁡(xik+1)(1))1≤k≤q\left(-T^{(1)}_{\xi(x_{i_{k}}),\xi(x_{i_{k}+1})}\right)_{1\leq k\leq q} connecting m′m^{\prime} to mm using −T(1)-T^{(1)}.
One should think of −T(1)-T^{(1)} as describing a macroscopic jump process between fibers, where the (ℒmac−K)(\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac}-K)-jump rates have been averaged under the microscopic measures (μm)1≤m≤M(\mu_{m})_{1\leq m\leq M}.
By the Perron–Frobenius theorem, we have a spectral gap
γs:=ℜ​𝔢​(η2(1)−η1(1))=ℜ​𝔢​(η2(1))−η1(1)>0,\gamma^{\rm s}:=\mathfrak{Re}\left(\eta_{2}^{(1)}-\eta_{1}^{(1)}\right)=\mathfrak{Re}\left(\eta_{2}^{(1)}\right)-\eta_{1}^{(1)}>0,
(218)
and positive principal eigenvectors
h0s=∑m=1Mαm​𝟙𝒳m∈P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳),ν0s=∑m=1Mβm​μm∈𝒫⁡(𝒳),h_{0}^{\rm s}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\alpha_{m}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m}}\in P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}),\qquad\nu_{0}^{\rm s}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\beta_{m}\mu_{m}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}),
(219)
such that α,β>0\alpha,\beta>0 pointwise, β∈𝒫⁡(𝒵)\beta\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Z}), and satisfying the eigenrelations and normalization convention
T(1)​h0s=η1(1)​h0s,ν0s​T(1)=η1(1)​ν0s,ν0s​(h0s)=1.T^{(1)}h_{0}^{\rm s}=\eta_{1}^{(1)}h_{0}^{\rm s},\qquad\nu_{0}^{\rm s}T^{(1)}=\eta_{1}^{(1)}\nu_{0}^{\rm s},\qquad\nu_{0}^{\rm s}(h_{0}^{\rm s})=1.
(220)
Since T(1)​𝟙𝒳=P0s​K​𝟙𝒳≥0T^{(1)}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=P_{0}^{\rm s}K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}\geq 0 has a strictly positive component (because KK is nonnegative and nonzero), we have ν0s​T(1)​𝟙𝒳=η1(1)=ν0s​(P0s​K​𝟙𝒳)>0\nu_{0}^{\rm s}T^{(1)}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=\eta_{1}^{(1)}=\nu_{0}^{\rm s}(P_{0}^{\rm s}K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}})>0.
We introduce two disjoint closed contours, Γ1s\Gamma_{1}^{\rm s} and Γ2s\Gamma_{2}^{\rm s}, enclosing −η1(1)-\eta_{1}^{(1)} and σ⁡(−T(1))∖{−η1(1)}\sigma(-T^{(1)})\setminus\{-\eta_{1}^{(1)}\} respectively, verifying
Γ2s⊂{z∈ℂ:ℜ​e​z<−η1(1)−γs/2}.\Gamma_{2}^{\rm s}\subset\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\,{\mathfrak{R}e}\,z<-\eta_{1}^{(1)}-\gamma^{\rm s}/2\right\}.
(221)
We also define these such that ε​Γ1s\varepsilon\Gamma_{1}^{\rm s} and ε​Γ2s\varepsilon\Gamma_{2}^{\rm s} are both enclosed by Γs\Gamma^{\rm s} for 0<ε<ε00<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}.
A schematic representation of the clustering of σ⁡(−T⁡(ε))\sigma(-T(\varepsilon)) by Γ1s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{1} and Γ2s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{2} is given in the right panel of Figure 18.
(a)ℜ​𝔢\mathfrak{Re}ℑ​𝔪\mathfrak{Im}Γs\Gamma^{\rm s}Γf\Gamma^{\rm f}γf\gamma^{\rm f}(b)ℜ​𝔢\mathfrak{Re}ℑ​𝔪\mathfrak{Im}Γs\Gamma^{\rm s}ε​Γ1s\varepsilon\Gamma_{1}^{\rm s}ε​Γ2s\varepsilon\Gamma_{2}^{\rm s}ε​γs\varepsilon\gamma^{\rm s}σ⁡(−T⁡(0))\sigma(-T(0))σ⁡(−T⁡(ε))\sigma(-T(\varepsilon)), ε∈(0,ε0)\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})σ⁡(−ε​T(1))\sigma(-\varepsilon T^{(1)})
Figure 18: Clustering of σ⁡(−T⁡(ε))\sigma(-T(\varepsilon)) (blue dots, see (202)). Black dots represent σ⁡(−T⁡(0))=σ⁡(ℒmic)\sigma(-T(0))=\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{\rm mic}). The right panel details the structure of the spectrum inside Γs\Gamma^{\rm s}, which comes from the probabilistic nature of T(1)T^{(1)} (see (213) and the Perron–Frobenius argument above).
Uniform version of Proposition 1.
We consider two successive perturbations, corresponding to the two levels of clustering introduced in the previous paragraph. Let UεsU^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon} denote Kato’s connection (207) associated with PεsP^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon} acting on ℓ∞​(𝒳)\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}). The conjugate
Tεs=ε−1​Uεs​T​(ε)​(Uεs)−1T^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-1}U^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}T(\varepsilon)\left(U^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}
(222)
has P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P^{\rm s}_{0}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}) as an invariant subspace, and restricted to this space has eigenvalues (ηj​(ε)/ε)1≤j≤M(\eta_{j}(\varepsilon)/\varepsilon)_{1\leq j\leq M}. By a direct computation (since P0s​T​(0)=T⁡(0)​P0s=0P_{0}^{\rm s}T(0)=T(0)P_{0}^{\rm s}=0) we have
‖Pεs​T​(0)​Pεs‖=‖(Pεs−P0s)​T​(0)​(Pεs−P0s)‖≤C​ε2,\|P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}T(0)P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}\|=\|(P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}-P^{\rm s}_{0})T(0)(P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}-P^{\rm s}_{0})\|\leq C\varepsilon^{2},
(223)
so that (206) and (207) together with the intertwining relations Uεs​Pεs=P0s​UεsU_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}=P_{0}^{\rm s}U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}, (Uεs)−1​P0s=Pεs​(Uεs)−1(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1}P_{0}^{\rm s}=P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1} give
‖(Tεs−T(1))​P0s‖\displaystyle\left\|\left(T^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}-T^{(1)}\right)P^{\rm s}_{0}\right\|
=‖ε−1​Uεs​Pεs​(T⁡(0)−ε​S)​Pεs​(Uεs)−1+P0s​S​P0s‖\displaystyle=\left\|\varepsilon^{-1}U^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}\left(T(0)-\varepsilon S\right)P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}\left(U^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{-1}+P^{\rm s}_{0}SP^{\rm s}_{0}\right\|
(224)
≤ε−1​‖Uεs​Pεs​T​(0)​Pεs​(Uεs)−1‖+‖P0s​S​P0s−Uεs​Pεs​S​Pεs​(Uεs)−1‖\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}T(0)P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1}\right\|+\left\|P_{0}^{\rm s}SP_{0}^{\rm s}-U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}SP_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1}\right\|
=ε−1​‖Uεs​Pεs​T​(0)​Pεs​(Uεs)−1‖+‖P0s​(S−Uεs​S​(Uεs)−1)​P0s‖\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}T(0)P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1}\right\|+\left\|P_{0}^{\rm s}(S-U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}S(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1})P_{0}^{\rm s}\right\|
≤ε−1​C​ε2+C​ε≤C​ε,\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1}C\varepsilon^{2}+C\varepsilon\leq C\varepsilon,
where we used (223) for the last inequality. That is, TεsT^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon} is a perturbation of T(1)T^{(1)} on P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}). Denote by Pεs,1P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s,1} the spectral projector associated to the Γ1s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{1}-part of σ⁡(−Tεs)\sigma(-T_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}), and Uεs,1:Pεs,1​ℓ∞​(𝒳)→P0s,1​ℓ∞​(𝒳)U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s,1}:P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s,1}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})\to P_{0}^{\rm{s},1}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}) the associated Kato connection, defined on P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}). We stress that Pεs,1P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s,1} is a projector on P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}), not Pεs​ℓ∞​(𝒳)P_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}).
Upon shrinking Γ1s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{1}, we may assume that Γ1s⊂{z∈ℂ:ℜ​𝔢​z>−η1(1)−γs/4}\Gamma^{\rm s}_{1}\subset\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\,\mathfrak{Re}\,z>-\eta^{(1)}_{1}-\gamma^{\rm s}/4\}. Since P0s,1=h0s​ν0sP^{{\rm s},1}_{0}=h^{\rm s}_{0}\nu^{\rm s}_{0} has rank one, so does Pεs,1P^{{\rm s},1}_{\varepsilon} for ε∈(0,ε0)\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}), and −Tεs-T^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon} has a unique, simple eigenvalue enclosed by Γ1s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{1}, which is necessarily the principal eigenvalue −λεX-\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon} of the similar operator ℒεX=−T(ε)/ε\mathcal{L}^{X}_{\varepsilon}=-T(\varepsilon)/\varepsilon, since all other eigenvalues of ℒεX\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{X} are enclosed by Γ2s\Gamma_{2}^{\rm s} or ε−1​Γf\varepsilon^{-1}\Gamma^{\rm f}.
In particular, λεX=η1​(ε)/ε<η1(1)+γs/4\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon}=\eta_{1}(\varepsilon)/\varepsilon<\eta^{(1)}_{1}+\gamma^{\rm s}/4.
We now write the principal spectral projector of ℒεX\mathcal{L}^{X}_{\varepsilon}, namely (Id−PεX)=hεX​νεX(\mathrm{Id}-P_{\varepsilon}^{X})=h^{X}_{\varepsilon}\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon}, via transport of P0s,1P_{0}^{\rm s,1} through the Kato connections, which gives
hεX​νεX=(Uεs)−1​(Uεs,1)−1​P0s,1​Uεs,1​Uεs​Pεs,h^{X}_{\varepsilon}\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon}=(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s})^{-1}(U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s,1})^{-1}P_{0}^{\rm s,1}U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s,1}U_{\varepsilon}^{\rm s}P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon},
(225)
so that (207) and P0s,1​P0s=h0s​ν0sP_{0}^{\rm s,1}P_{0}^{\rm s}=h_{0}^{\rm s}\nu_{0}^{\rm s} give ‖hεX​νεX−h0s​ν0s‖≤C​ε\|h_{\varepsilon}^{X}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{0}^{\rm s}\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\|\leq C\varepsilon.
Since νεX​𝟙𝒳=ν0s​𝟙𝒳=1\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=1, we have ‖hεX−h0s‖≤‖hεX​νεX−h0s​ν0s‖​‖𝟙𝒳‖≤C​ε\|h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{0}^{\rm s}\|\leq\|h_{\varepsilon}^{X}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{0}^{\rm s}\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\|\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}\|\leq C\varepsilon.
Therefore, |ν0s​hεX−ν0s​h0s|≤C​ε|\nu_{0}^{\rm s}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\nu_{0}^{\rm s}h_{0}^{\rm s}|\leq C\varepsilon, and writing νεX=ν0s​hεX​νεXν0s​hεX\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}=\frac{\nu_{0}^{\rm s}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}}{\nu_{0}^{\rm s}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}}, we get
‖νεX−ν0s‖∗≤‖ν0s‖∗​‖hεX​νεXν0s​hεX−h0s​ν0s‖≤C​ε,\|\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\|_{*}\leq\|\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\|_{*}\left\|\frac{h_{\varepsilon}^{X}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}}{\nu_{0}^{\rm s}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}}-h_{0}^{\rm s}\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\right\|\leq C\varepsilon,
(226)
which together give
‖hεX−h0s‖+‖νεX−ν0s‖∗≤C​ε,min𝒳⁡hεX≥12​min1≤m≤M​αm.\|h^{X}_{\varepsilon}-h^{\rm s}_{0}\|+\|\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon}-\nu^{\rm s}_{0}\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon,\qquad\min_{\mathcal{X}}h^{X}_{\varepsilon}\geq\frac{1}{2}\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\alpha_{m}.
(227)
The lower bound comes from the explicit expression (219) for h0sh_{0}^{\rm s}.
Since ℒmic,ℒmac\mathcal{L}_{\rm mic},\mathcal{L}_{\rm mac} are mass-conserving, ℒεX​𝟙𝒳=−K​𝟙𝒳\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{X}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=-K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}, so that λεX=νεX​(K​𝟙𝒳)\lambda_{\varepsilon}^{X}=\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}(K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}). Similarly, since P0s​𝟙𝒳=𝟙𝒳P_{0}^{\rm s}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}, λ0s=η1(1)=ν0s​(P0s​K​𝟙𝒳)=ν0s​(K​𝟙𝒳)\lambda_{0}^{\rm s}=\eta_{1}^{(1)}=\nu_{0}^{\rm s}(P_{0}^{\rm s}K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}})=\nu_{0}^{\rm s}(K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}) (recall KK is a diagonal operator), so that
|λεX−λ0s|≤‖νεX−ν0s‖∗​‖K​𝟙𝒳‖≤C​ε,|\lambda_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\lambda_{0}^{\rm s}|\leq\|\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\nu_{0}^{\rm s}\|_{*}\|K\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}}\|\leq C\varepsilon,
(228)
from (227). This shows the second inequality in (123).
Finally, using (208), we may write PεX=Id−hεX​νεX=Pεf+(Uεs)−1​Pεs,2​Uεs​PεsP^{X}_{\varepsilon}=\mathrm{Id}-h^{X}_{\varepsilon}\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon}=P^{\rm f}_{\varepsilon}+(U^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon})^{-1}P^{{\rm s},2}_{\varepsilon}U^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}P^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon}.
Applying the semigroup estimate (209) to −T⁡(ε)-T(\varepsilon) with Γf\Gamma^{\rm f} at time t/εt/\varepsilon, and to −Tεs-T^{\rm s}_{\varepsilon} with Γ2s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{2} at time tt, we get
∀t≥0,∥Qt,εXPεX∥≤C(e−γft/(4ε)+e−(η1(1)+γs/2)​t)≤Ce−(λεX+γs/4)​t\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\|Q^{X}_{t,\varepsilon}P^{X}_{\varepsilon}\|\leq C\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma^{\rm f}t/(4\varepsilon)}+\mathrm{e}^{-(\eta^{(1)}_{1}+\gamma^{\rm s}/2)t}\right)\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon}+\gamma^{\rm s}/4)t}
(229)
for ε\varepsilon small enough. This verifies the estimate (18) from Proposition 1 with a uniform spectral gap γεX=γs/4≥1/C\gamma^{X}_{\varepsilon}=\gamma^{\rm s}/4\geq 1/C and prefactor Cγ,εX≤CC^{X}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}\leq C uniformly in ε∈(0,ε0)\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}).
Decomposing the semigroup Qt,εX=e−λεX​t​hεX​νεX+Qt,εX​PεXQ^{X}_{t,\varepsilon}=\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon}t}h^{X}_{\varepsilon}\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon}+Q^{X}_{t,\varepsilon}P^{X}_{\varepsilon}, we then estimate using the convergence hεX​νεX→h0s​ν0sh_{\varepsilon}^{X}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}\to h_{0}^{\rm s}\nu_{0}^{\rm s}
∀t≥0,‖Qt,εX‖≤C​e−λεX​t,\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\|Q^{X}_{t,\varepsilon}\|\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon}t},
(230)
which proves the estimate (15) from Proposition 1 with a uniform prefactor Cλ,εX≤CC^{X}_{\lambda,\varepsilon}\leq C.
Note this argument could be optimized to obtain a sharper spectral gap.
Convergence of ΠεX\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}.
Define Π0Z:ℓ∞​(𝒳)→ℓ∞​(𝒵)\Pi^{Z}_{0}:\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})\to\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}) by Π0Z​f​(m)=μm​(f)\Pi^{Z}_{0}f(m)=\mu_{m}(f) for m∈𝒵m\in\mathcal{Z}, so that P0s=ξ⋆​Π0ZP^{\rm s}_{0}=\xi^{\star}\Pi^{Z}_{0} (see (214)). Since ν0s=∑mβm​μm\nu^{\rm s}_{0}=\sum_{m}\beta_{m}\mu_{m}, the operator Π0Z\Pi^{Z}_{0} is defined by the disintegration of ν0s\nu^{\rm s}_{0} with respect to ξ\xi, just as ΠεZ\Pi^{Z}_{\varepsilon} was defined by the disintegration of νεX\nu^{X}_{\varepsilon} with respect to ξ\xi (see (39)).
By the convergence (227), νεX​𝟙𝒳m→ν0s​𝟙𝒳m=βm>0\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m}}\to\nu^{\rm s}_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{m}}=\beta_{m}>0 (see (219)), and ‖νεX​f−ν0s​f‖≤C​ε​‖f‖\|\nu_{\varepsilon}^{X}f-\nu_{0}^{\rm s}f\|\leq C\varepsilon\|f\|, we obtain
‖ΠεZ−Π0Z‖+‖ΠεX−P0s‖≤(1+‖ξ⋆‖)​‖ΠεZ−Π0Z‖≤C​ε,\|\Pi^{Z}_{\varepsilon}-\Pi^{Z}_{0}\|+\|\Pi^{X}_{\varepsilon}-P^{\rm s}_{0}\|\leq(1+\|\xi^{\star}\|)\|\Pi^{Z}_{\varepsilon}-\Pi^{Z}_{0}\|\leq C\varepsilon,
(231)
which proves the first estimate in (123).
Separation of timescales and uniform closure defect.
Let Πε⟂=Id−ΠεX\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}=\mathrm{Id}-\Pi^{X}_{\varepsilon}, and consider the operators Hε=ε​Πε⟂​ℒεX​Πε⟂H_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}^{X}_{\varepsilon}\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}. Since ΠεX​ℓ∞​(𝒳)⊂P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)\Pi^{X}_{\varepsilon}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})\subset P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}) (ΠεX\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X} is a fiber-averaging operator), we have R​ΠεX=0R\Pi^{X}_{\varepsilon}=0. Using P0s​R=0P^{\rm s}_{0}R=0 and (231), we estimate
‖Hε−R‖\displaystyle\|H_{\varepsilon}-R\|
≤‖(Id−ΠεX)​R​(Id−ΠεX)−R‖+ε​‖(Id−ΠεX)​S​(Id−ΠεX)‖\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})R(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})-R\right\|+\varepsilon\|(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})S(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})\|
(232)
≤‖(Id−ΠεX)​R−R‖+C​ε\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})R-R\right\|+C\varepsilon
=‖(P0s−ΠεX)​R‖+C​ε\displaystyle=\left\|(P_{0}^{\rm s}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})R\right\|+C\varepsilon
≤C​ε.\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon.
That is, HεH_{\varepsilon} is a perturbation of RR vanishing on the MM-dimensional space ΠεX​ℓ∞​(𝒳)\Pi^{X}_{\varepsilon}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}) and leaving Πε⟂​ℓ∞​(𝒳)\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}) invariant. By perturbation theory, HεH_{\varepsilon} has exactly MM eigenvalues enclosed by Γs\Gamma^{\rm s}, which are therefore its zero eigenvalues on ΠεX​ℓ∞​(𝒳)\Pi^{X}_{\varepsilon}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}), and the spectral projector of HεH_{\varepsilon} associated with Γf\Gamma^{\rm f} is Πε⟂\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon} (take Γ\Gamma such that Pε​(Γ)=IdP_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)=\mathrm{Id} in (208)). Applying the semigroup estimate (209) at time t/εt/\varepsilon then gives
∀t≥0,‖et​Πε⟂​ℒεX​Πε⟂Πε⟂‖≤Ce−γft/(2ε),\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\left\|\mathrm{e}^{t\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}^{X}_{\varepsilon}\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}}\Pi^{\perp}_{\varepsilon}\right\|\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma^{\rm f}t/(2\varepsilon)},
(233)
which verifies (51) with ζε=γf/(2​ε)≥1/(C​ε)\zeta_{\varepsilon}=\gamma^{\rm f}/(2\varepsilon)\geq 1/(C\varepsilon) and Cζ,ε≤CC_{\zeta,\varepsilon}\leq C uniformly in ε∈(0,ε0)\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}). In particular, ζε>2​(λεX+γεX)\zeta_{\varepsilon}>2(\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon}+\gamma^{X}_{\varepsilon}) for ε<ε0\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0} small enough, and the assumptions of Theorem 5 have been verified.
To control AεXA_{\varepsilon}^{X}, we perform a similar computation, writing
AεX\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}^{X}
=ℒεX​ΠεX−ΠεX​ℒεX\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{X}\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{X}
(234)
=(ε−1​R+S)​ΠεX−ΠεX​(ε−1​R+S)\displaystyle=(\varepsilon^{-1}R+S)\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}(\varepsilon^{-1}R+S)
=(S​ΠεX−ΠεX​S)+ε−1​(P0s−ΠεX)​R.\displaystyle=(S\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}S)+\varepsilon^{-1}(P_{0}^{\rm s}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X})R.
A triangle inequality together with the convergence (231) then yields ‖AεX‖≤‖S​ΠεX−ΠεX​S‖+ε−1​C​ε≤C\|A_{\varepsilon}^{X}\|\leq\|S\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}-\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{X}S\|+\varepsilon^{-1}C\varepsilon\leq C uniformly in ε∈(0,ε0)\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}).
In particular, the approximation parameter in Theorem 5 satisfies ‖AεX‖/ζε≤C​ε\|A^{X}_{\varepsilon}\|/\zeta_{\varepsilon}\leq C\varepsilon.
Uniform estimates for ZεZ_{\varepsilon}.
Since R​ξ⋆=0R\xi^{\star}=0 on ℓ∞​(𝒵)\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}), the generator (40) of the canonical model is given by ℒεZ=ΠεZ​S​ξ⋆\mathcal{L}^{Z}_{\varepsilon}=\Pi^{Z}_{\varepsilon}S\xi^{\star}, and by (231), ‖ℒεZ−ℒ0Z‖≤C​ε\|\mathcal{L}^{Z}_{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{L}_{0}^{Z}\|\leq C\varepsilon, where ℒ0Z=Π0Z​S​ξ⋆\mathcal{L}_{0}^{Z}=\Pi^{Z}_{0}S\xi^{\star} is conjugated with −T(1)-T^{(1)} via the isomorphism ξ⋆:ℓ∞​(𝒵)→P0s​ℓ∞​(𝒳)\xi^{\star}:\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z})\to P_{0}^{\rm s}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}). One can simply check that ℒ0Z\mathcal{L}_{0}^{Z} has principal eigenvectors α∈ℓ∞​(𝒵)\alpha\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}) and β∈𝒫⁡(𝒵)\beta\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Z}), where we recall (219).
By perturbation theory and the clustering of σ⁡(−T(1))\sigma(-T^{(1)}) from above, ℒεZ\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{Z} has exactly one simple eigenvalue enclosed in Γ1s\Gamma_{1}^{\rm s}, with the rest of the spectrum enclosed by Γ2s\Gamma_{2}^{\rm s} for ε∈(0,ε0)\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}).
Denote PεZ,1=hεZ​νεZP_{\varepsilon}^{Z,1}=h_{\varepsilon}^{Z}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{Z} and PεZ,2P_{\varepsilon}^{Z,2} the associated spectral projectors, satisfying Id=PεZ,1+PεZ,2\mathrm{Id}=P_{\varepsilon}^{Z,1}+P_{\varepsilon}^{Z,2} on ℓ∞​(𝒵)\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}). In particular PεZ=PεZ,2P_{\varepsilon}^{Z}=P_{\varepsilon}^{Z,2}.
Applying (206), we get ‖hεZ​νεZ−α​β‖≤C​ε\|h_{\varepsilon}^{Z}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{Z}-\alpha\beta\|\leq C\varepsilon, and reasoning as for (227), we get
‖hεZ−α‖+‖νεZ−β‖∗≤C​ε,min𝒵⁡hεZ≥12​min1≤m≤M​αm.\|h^{Z}_{\varepsilon}-\alpha\|+\|\nu^{Z}_{\varepsilon}-\beta\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon,\qquad\min_{\mathcal{Z}}h^{Z}_{\varepsilon}\geq\frac{1}{2}\min_{1\leq m\leq M}\alpha_{m}.
(235)
Applying the semigroup estimate (209) (as we did for (229), but without the contribution of Γf\Gamma^{\rm f}) to ℒεZ\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}^{Z} with Γ2s\Gamma^{\rm s}_{2}, we obtain
∀t≥0,‖Qt,εZ​PεZ‖≤C​e−(λεX+γs/4)​t=C​e−(λεZ+γs/4)​t,\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\|Q^{Z}_{t,\varepsilon}P^{Z}_{\varepsilon}\|\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\lambda^{X}_{\varepsilon}+\gamma^{\rm s}/4)t}=C\mathrm{e}^{-(\lambda^{Z}_{\varepsilon}+\gamma^{\rm s}/4)t},
(236)
and decomposing Qt,εZ=e−λεZ​t​hεZ​νεZ+Qt,εZ​PεZQ^{Z}_{t,\varepsilon}=\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda^{Z}_{\varepsilon}t}h_{\varepsilon}^{Z}\nu_{\varepsilon}^{Z}+Q^{Z}_{t,\varepsilon}P^{Z}_{\varepsilon} as for (230) yields
∀t≥0,‖Qt,εZ‖≤C​e−λεZ​t,\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\|Q^{Z}_{t,\varepsilon}\|\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda_{\varepsilon}^{Z}t},
(237)
which verifies Proposition 1 for ZεZ_{\varepsilon}, with a uniform spectral gap γεZ≥1/C\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{Z}\geq 1/C, and uniform prefactors Cλ,εZ,Cγ,εZ≤CC_{\lambda,\varepsilon}^{Z},C_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{Z}\leq C.
This finishes the proof of (124).
hεYh^{Y}_{\varepsilon}-norm estimates and dependence on η0X\eta_{0}^{X}.
We finally prove the last two estimates in (123) and the estimates (125).
We start with (123). For Y=X,ZY=X,Z, notice that
|min𝒴​ΠεY​hεXhεY−1|=|min𝒴​ΠεY​hεX−hεYhεY|≤(min𝒴​hεY)−1​‖ΠεY​hεX−hεY‖\displaystyle\left|\underset{\mathcal{Y}}{\min}\,\frac{\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{Y}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}}{h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}}-1\right|=\left|\underset{\mathcal{Y}}{\min}\,\frac{\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{Y}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}}{h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}}\right|\leq\left(\underset{\mathcal{Y}}{\min}\,h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}\right)^{-1}\|\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{Y}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}\|
(238)
≤C​‖ΠεY​hεX−hεY‖,\displaystyle\leq C\|\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{Y}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}\|,
where we use the last estimate in either (227) or (235) in the last line. We then use the quasimode estimates (53) and (54), together with (124) to control the constants, and conclude that
|min𝒴​ΠεY​hεXhεY−1|≤C​ε,\left|\underset{\mathcal{Y}}{\min}\,\frac{\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{Y}h_{\varepsilon}^{X}}{h_{\varepsilon}^{Y}}-1\right|\leq C\varepsilon,
(239)
which finishes the proof of (123).
Finally, we verify (125).
We write
|η0X​(hεX)−η0X​(h0s)|≤‖η0X‖∗​‖hεX−h0s‖=‖hεX−h0s‖≤C​ε\left|\eta_{0}^{X}(h_{\varepsilon}^{X})-\eta_{0}^{X}(h_{0}^{\rm s})\right|\leq\|\eta_{0}^{X}\|_{*}\|h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{0}^{\rm s}\|=\|h_{\varepsilon}^{X}-h_{0}^{\rm s}\|\leq C\varepsilon
(240)
using (227), and using the compatibility condition η0Z=ξ⋆​η0X\eta_{0}^{Z}=\xi_{\star}\eta_{0}^{X},
|η0Z​(hεZ)−η0X​(h0s)|=|η0Z​(hεZ)−η0X​(ξ⋆​α)|≤‖η0Z‖∗​‖hεZ−α‖=‖hεZ−α‖≤C​ε,\left|\eta_{0}^{Z}(h_{\varepsilon}^{Z})-\eta_{0}^{X}(h_{0}^{\rm s})\right|=\left|\eta_{0}^{Z}(h_{\varepsilon}^{Z})-\eta_{0}^{X}(\xi^{\star}\alpha)\right|\leq\|\eta_{0}^{Z}\|_{*}\|h_{\varepsilon}^{Z}-\alpha\|=\|h_{\varepsilon}^{Z}-\alpha\|\leq C\varepsilon,
(241)
using (235). Finally, the lower bound η0X​(h0s)≥1/C\eta_{0}^{X}(h_{0}^{\rm s})\geq 1/C follows from the strict positivity of α\alpha.
Appendix B Implementation details
B.1 Synthetic prior construction
We describe the hand-crafted synthetic prior used in the experiments of Section 4. All random draws (indicated by ∼\sim) appearing in this paragraph are independent.
A sample from ρdyn\rho_{\rm dyn} is specified by random physical parameters UU, DD and a random inverse temperature β∼𝒰⁡([βmin,βmax])\beta\sim\mathcal{U}([\beta_{\min},\beta_{\max}]).
The energy function UU is given by first constructing the random function
U~​(z)=1−e−(z−c)22​κ2+∑i=1mUhi​e−(z−ci,U)22​κi,U2+b​z,\widetilde{U}(z)=1-\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{(z-c)^{2}}{2\kappa^{2}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{m_{U}}h_{i}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{(z-c_{i,U})^{2}}{2\kappa_{i,U}^{2}}}+bz,
(242)
composed of a Gaussian well centered at c∼𝒰⁡([0.15,0.85]){c\sim\mathcal{U}([0.15,0.85])} and of width κ∼𝒰⁡([0.2,0.6]){\kappa\sim\mathcal{U}([0.2,0.6])}, perturbed by mU∼𝒰⁡{1,…,6}{m_{U}\sim\mathcal{U}\{1,\dots,6\}} internal energy barriers, each given, for 1≤i≤mU{1\leq i\leq m_{U}}, by a Gaussian profile centered at ci,U∼𝒰⁡([0,1]){c_{i,U}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1])},
of height hi∼𝒰⁡([0,δmax]){h_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,\delta_{\max}])} and of width κi,U=κi,U′/mU{\kappa_{i,U}}=\kappa_{i,U}^{\prime}/m_{U}, where κi,U′∼𝒰⁡([0.2,0.6]){\kappa_{i,U}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{U}([0.2,0.6])}.
A linear correction term b​zbz is added to enforce |U~​(1)−U~​(0)|=δE∼𝒰⁡([0,δmax]){|\widetilde{U}(1)-\widetilde{U}(0)|=\delta_{E}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,\delta_{\max}])}.
Then, UU is constructed using an affine transformation of U~\widetilde{U} to enforce U⁡([0,1])=[0,1]{U([0,1])=[0,1]}. Setting a fixed energy scale is motivated by the fact that the parameter β\beta modulates the height of energy barriers relative to the thermal energy of the system.
The diffusion coefficient DD is constructed by first drawing a constant diffusion σ=es{\sigma=\mathrm{e}^{s}} where s∼𝒰⁡([smin,smax]){s\sim\mathcal{U}([s_{\min},s_{\max}])}, which is either kept constant with probability 1/21/2 (in which case D⁡(z)=σD(z)=\sigma), or else perturbed by mD∼𝒰⁡{1,…,6}m_{D}\sim\mathcal{U}\{1,\dots,6\} Gaussian profiles
D⁡(z)=σ+q⁡(z)−min⁡q,q⁡(z)=∑i=1mDσ​gi​e−(z−ci,D)22​(κi,D)2,D(z)=\sigma+q(z)-\min q,\qquad q(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{D}}\sigma g_{i}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{(z-c_{i,D})^{2}}{2(\kappa_{i,D})^{2}}},
(243)
with gi∼𝒩⁡(0,1)g_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1), ci,D∼𝒰⁡([0,1])c_{i,D}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]) and κi,D=κi,D′/mD\kappa_{i,D}=\kappa_{i,D}^{\prime}/m_{D}, κi,D′∼𝒰⁡([0.2,0.6])\kappa_{i,D}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{U}([0.2,0.6]), so that min[0,1]⁡D=σ\min_{[0,1]}D=\sigma.
The prior ρ0\rho_{0} is specified by sampling Z0∼𝒰⁡(𝒵)Z_{0}\sim\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}) uniformly on the grid 𝒵⊂(0,1)\mathcal{Z}\subset(0,1).
The various (training and misspecified) priors of Section 4.3 differ in the ranges [βmin,βmax]{[\beta_{\min},\beta_{\max}]}, [smin,smax]{[s_{\min},s_{\max}]} and δmax\delta_{\max}.
All the other parameter distributions entering (242) and (243) are fixed.
The parameter ranges used in the experiments of Section 4.3 are given in Table 2.

Instructions for reporting errors
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
 support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
 methods listed below:
Click the "Report Issue" (
 ) button, located in the page header.
Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.
Our team has already identified the following issues. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
 may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
 should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
 all.
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion, and welcome developer contributions.
 We gratefully acknowledge support from our major funders,
 member institutions, ,
 and all contributors.
